The generator builds a canonical document: sections in fixed order (identity, what exists, what it may do, what it may not do, what is broken, what is prophetic, how to rebuild), records in sorted key order, timestamps rounded to the generator's freshness bucket (for example the hour) so that unchanged state yields identical bytes, and statements annotated with their class (A to D of 52.2), evidence references and, for class A, the observed value. The document is rendered in two forms from the same records: a structured form (JSON) for agents and a text form (Markdown) for people. A neutral renderer function holds no logic about content.

[0422] Step 3010: diff. The generator reads the committed copy from the repository default branch and computes a record-level diff (added, removed, changed statements) and a text diff. Changes in observed values that stay inside a tolerance band (for example a count that moved by less than 5 percent, where a quantity is volatile) may be rendered as a bucketed value so that the repository is not churned by noise; the bucket width is a stated parameter.

[0423] Step 3012: consistency check. The check of 53.3 runs on the assembled records. Step 3014: on failure, the offending statement is moved to the honest-gap list with class "unverified" or a work item is opened carrying the statement, the missing evidence and a closing probe, and the generator proceeds with the rendering that results; the check never silently deletes a statement.

[0424] Step 3016: if the diff is empty the generator records a clean generation in the tick report (step 3022) and stops. Step 3018: otherwise it opens a change on a branch named for the generator, with the record-level diff and the probe evidence in the description, assigned to a reviewer that is a different agent from the generator (and, where two exist, a different model, per section 12). Step 3020: when the review passes and checks are green, the change merges by the canonical change path (section 12), and a row is appended to the doctrine lineage (`doctrine_lineage`: version, parent, changed_records, reviewer, merged_at). After merge, the repository text equals the running system as of the generation time.

[0425] Two directions of drift are distinguished. If the diff arises from the system (a belief falsified, a capability added), the system is the source and the repository is updated. If the diff arises from an edit to the committed copy by a human or agent that does not match the records, the generator does not overwrite it silently: it opens an item "repository text differs from system" with the hunk, because either the edit expresses an intended change to be translated into a record through the reviewed path, or the text is wrong. A rule "the records are the source and the text is a rendering" is stated in the document header; a hand edit must be made to the records. Alternative embodiments: the committed copy may be generated only (a check on pull requests fails when the file differs from a fresh generation, as with a lockfile); or the committed copy may be hand-edited prose with a generated block between markers, the way the seed's protocol block is managed by the seed between start and end markers and edited only in the seed.

[0426] Worked example (illustrative). Hour 14:17: the generator reads 7 product claims; 7 probes return `ok`; one belief probe "no silent drop" returns `ok` with detail "0 drops"; the boundary check returns live 40, unclassified 0. The rendered text equals the committed copy except for "latest tick 13:50" versus "latest tick 14:10", which bucket to the same hour, so the diff is empty: step 3022. At hour 15:17 a deploy-probe belief returns "failed" and the claim `tick-running` returns "no tick in 2 hours". The check passes (both have evidence); the diff has two changed statements; step 3018 opens a change with those two hunks; a reviewer merges; lineage row appended. Technical effect: the document is never more than one generation interval plus one review interval stale, and its staleness is itself stated at the top ("generated at, from commit").

### 53.2 Claims in the description carry probes

[0427] The product-claims pattern of section 44.3 is applied to the self-description itself. Each statement of class A or B is written as a row with a single-SELECT probe, so that the sentence "the system classifies every live worker" is stored with a probe over the boundary check's output table, and "predictions are scored" with the 7-day outcome count probe. The text that humans read is generated from the rows, and a statement that no probe can support is class D (prophetic) or goes to the honest-gap list. The failure recorded on 2026-11-10 in section 44.3, in which four probes held a semicolon in a text literal and the page read 3 of 7 until a follow-up migration, is a worked reason to validate each probe at write time with the same runner that will execute it; the generator therefore rejects at assembly any probe that the runner would refuse, as a step 3006 error and not at view time. Status: built and operating for product claims; extension to every statement prophetic.

### 53.3 Consistency check

[0428] The consistency check, run at step 3012, applies the following rules and fails on the first violation of any, collecting all violations for the work items.

| Rule | Test | Example violation |
|---|---|---|
| E1 evidence present | every class A, B, C statement has a resolvable `evidence_ref` or probe | a capability with no commit or probe |
| E2 evidence fresh | class A evidence `checked_at` is within its freshness limit | a ledger refresh older than 3 hours |
| E3 no value of a secret | no field value matches a secret pattern; access map values are booleans | a token in a note field |
| E4 no future in the present | a class D statement is not rendered in a section that says "can do" | a prophetic feature listed as a capability |
| E5 limits not widened | the embedded ceilings and rungs equal the policy file's | a rung higher than the policy's |
| E6 boundary clean | boundary check has zero violations | an unclassified worker |
| E7 gaps probeable | every gap has a closing probe or the label unprobeable with a principal card | a gap with none |
| E8 prophetic carries responsible role | class D statements name a responsible role and a trigger | none named |
| E9 harness mirrors policy | each gated action reachable by a shell command has a deny rule | the existing test, section 19A |

[0429] Rules E5 and E9 are the self-description's tie to authority: a description cannot say an agent may do more than the policy allows, and the description never changes a ceiling, a rung or a limit; those change only by reviewed change to the policy (section 19A). Status: E6 and E9 built and operating as separate checks; E1 to E5, E7 and E8 prophetic. Technical effect: a statement of ability without support is a failing check; the count of unsupported statements is zero in a passing build, and a self-claim of the kind "the system can provision a fresh account" cannot appear in the passing document until a probe supports it.

### 53.4 Amendment path and lineage

[0430] Amendments to the record set (a new belief, a new limit, a changed gap) go through the reviewed change path. A change by the generator needs a different agent as reviewer. A change that lowers a class (for example from A to D) requires the reason in the change description. A change that edits standing limits or the autonomy policy additionally requires the principal's approval in words attached to the change, because the description does not widen authority (rule E5). Each merged change appends a lineage row with `version`, `parent`, `changed_records` and `reviewer`. Repeated failure patterns (for example three of the same failure within seven days, per the seed's rule for role changes) produce proposed amendments to records or to role files. Status: the reviewed change path and lineage are built and operating (sections 11, 12, 23); generator-authored amendments are prophetic.

## 54. Use of the self-description and self-bootstrapping (FIG. 31)

### 54.1 Loading by a new session

[0431] "Session" means a bounded run of an agent process that holds no memory of prior runs, as in the seed's rule that no agent depends on another's chat history. Step 3102: a session starts with only the repository (or a seed copy of it). Step 3104: it reads the protocol file, its role file and the policy, and then the self-description. Step 3106: it derives (i) its permission ceiling per surface and its rung, (ii) the gates, (iii) the standing limits, (iv) what exists (components, capabilities), (v) what is broken (falsified beliefs, failing claims, open gaps), and (vi) what is prophetic and must not be relied on. In the first record the bootstrap path for a fresh session is five steps: read the protocol, role and policy; read the index, handoff, roadmap and open items and claim the highest-priority item; work on a branch with a draft change and four-line reporting; check each fact against its source before writing it; propose one plus per closed item. Status: built as a practice using the hand-written record and the index in the reference implementation; loading a generated description is prophetic.

[0432] Rule for use: the session treats the description as data with a freshness stamp. When the stamp is older than a limit (for example 24 hours) or a probe in the document fails, the session re-runs the probes it relies on before acting. Prompt injection note: because the description is derived from records, it carries no instructions to agents; the loader renders it in a data block and the harness's checks and the policy win over any sentence in it. (This follows the kernel's reading that the kernel is not a source of authority.)

### 54.2 Manager tick consults the description

[0433] In one embodiment step 3114 of the tick (the claim and dispatch steps of the seed's seven-step tick, or the twenty-step tick of section 24) calls a function `may(surface, action_class, persona)` that reads the description's embedded policy and returns one of allow, allow with rung condition, propose only, or deny with reason. Dispatch of an item whose doer line targets a surface where the current rung is below the needed rung is routed to a reviewer role (rungs observed, assisted: a reviewer agent executes or merges) as in the ladder meaning of the policy. A denied item is recorded with its reason, the next item is taken (step 3116), and no workaround of the check is tried: this implements the standing rule that a refusal is an input. A prediction is logged before acting, and the action's provenance records the description version consulted. Status: built for the policy lookup in the seed's validator and the tick's dispatch order; the version stamp on provenance is prophetic.

[0434] Technical effect: the same data that a person reads determines what the tick does, so a description that is wrong produces a visibly wrong behavior that a probe catches, and a description cannot be a documentation-only artifact.

### 54.3 Bootstrapper emission for a new tenant

[0435] At bootstrapper stages 1508 to 1514 (plan, write, provision, start, section 19A), the planner emits for a new tenant a first self-description generated from the tenant's own policy, role set, kit backlog and detect results: access map from stage 1504, surfaces and ceilings from the policy, an honest-gap list initialized with every stage that has not run (for example "provision: not run", "six complete ticks: not seen"), and class D statements for all other capabilities. The kit item that closes only when six complete ticks are seen in one hour is the closing probe of the gap "heartbeat not armed". As stages complete, their gaps close by probe and the next generation of the description reflects it. A tenant therefore starts with a self-description that says honestly what it cannot yet do. Status: prophetic as emission (the seed writes policy, org files and a report today; it does not write a description).

### 54.4 Reconstruction after loss

[0436] Scenario 1, a wiped machine: the principal's local folder is lost. The repository holds the protocol, role files, policy, roadmap, issues, decisions, handoff and the self-description; a new session clones, performs 54.1 and continues; the unpushed work of a prior session is lost, which is why checkpoint commits after each stage are a rule of the protocol. Scenario 2, a fresh cloud account: the seed, the idea file and the self-description (as the source of the organization's roles, outcomes and policy) feed the bootstrapper, which creates the runtime (a database, a worker and a schedule), applies migrations, executes seed rows with insert-or-ignore semantics so that re-running is safe, and starts the heartbeat; the description and the repository then supply the rest of the state. The idempotent re-run is built; an end-to-end reconstruction of an organization onto a fresh cloud account from the repository has not been run. Scenario 3, loss of the database: state that exists only in tables (trust ledger, prediction outcomes) is restored from backups; state that exists as repository text (policy, roles, roadmap, description) is restored by sync (54.5). Status: prophetic as a measured procedure; the components are built and tested offline.

[0437] Rule "if it is not in code it does not exist": every decision, plan and result lands as a commit, an issue, a pull request or a database row in the same turn it is made, and a chat is never the only record. A fact that exists only in a chat is therefore absent from the description by construction. In one implementation a handoff file and the index are updated on every session stop so that a cold agent can continue; the first day produced a hand-written index of where each class of artifact lives (roadmap in milestones, tasks in issues, intellectual property in a register, hand-off in a state file) and an hourly workflow that regenerates a grouping of issues by area and a register of intellectual property from the issue tracker, committing only if the content changed, then runs a verification script.

### 54.5 Sync jobs

[0438] "Sync job" means a scheduled procedure that makes two copies of a record agree under a stated direction. Three sync directions: (a) tracker to repository (built: the index workflow, hourly and on issue events, with the output committed under a bot identity); (b) repository to database: policy, role files and description are loaded into rows by the tick's reconcile step, and a database row that differs from the repository text is reported as drift and the repository wins for policy and roles (prophetic as a general rule; the seed writes policy to the database at provisioning); (c) database to repository: operational state worth keeping in git (a summary of ledgers) is exported on a schedule as a generated file (the generator of 53.1). In each direction the sync job records the source commit and time, never deletes the destination without a disposition, and on a conflict opens an item and leaves both copies. Alternative embodiments: sync by a webhook event instead of a clock; two-way sync with a vector of last-writer per record; or a content-addressed store with signatures.

### 54.6 Self-hosting: the platform's backlog as tenant number one

[0439] Status: prophetic (not yet built) as a tenant; built as a practice by hand. In one embodiment the platform's own roadmap, written as an idea file and an issue backlog, is run through the same seed as any tenant (section 45.3), so that the self-description of the platform is the generated description of tenant number one. The sessions that built the first instance already worked as the product's own first managed project: the repository was the memory, sessions were disposable, and the roadmap was a milestone and issue set; the principal's decisions, the product design and the drafting were recorded as files. What is not built is the platform's backlog running on a separately provisioned tenant plane. The self-description for that tenant lists "platform backlog hosted on its own tenant" in the honest-gap list until the closing probe (a second installation produces six complete ticks in an hour and claims one platform item) passes. Technical effect: the product improves through the loops it sells, and its own claims about itself are tested by the same probes as any customer's.

### 54.7 Honest self-knowledge as a measured property

[0440] Four numbers are tracked per generation: statements by class (A, B, C, D), statements without evidence (target zero), falsified beliefs with age in hours, and gaps with closing probe versus unprobeable. In one embodiment a report line "self-knowledge" is added to the four-line report: what the description says changed, which statements newly failed, which gaps closed, what is open. Trust events reward honesty: a statement moved from B to D by the system's own check, before an outside reader found it, is recorded as a clean self-remediation (weight as in section 24.3), and a statement found false by an outside reader is a hidden failure on the surface "self-description", demoting its rung and opening a failure-mode register class. Status: prophetic for the metrics; the trust event types (clean self-remediation, capability gap closed) exist in the kernel in the reference implementation.

[0441] Limits of the mechanism. The description can state that a probe passed; it cannot establish that the probe measures what the statement means. The kernel's measures of decision accuracy are scored by the system itself, and the first record says there is no outside audit. The mechanism therefore treats an outside reader's correction as the strongest evidence, and the review of a description change by a different agent and model family reduces, but does not remove, correlated error. Statements of this kind are themselves carried in the honest-gap list.

## 55. Business function chain: registries, dependencies and gates (FIG. 32)

[0442] This chapter extends the organization kit of section 19, the bootstrapping of section 19A and the seed of section 43 from a development platform to an autonomous business system. "Business function" means a recurring kind of work that a business needs and that has a managing persona, a defined set of output artifacts, a done check and a gate class; examples are product development, intellectual property work, marketing, promotion, events, funding, branding, legal and entity decisions, web presence and reporting. "Function chain" means the set of business functions of one organization together with the dependency edges between them. "Artifact" means a durable output of a work item that is stored as a file under version control (a document, a figure, a specification, a configuration, a site) and that has a registered status. The chapter's technical effect is that a single idea file is turned, by data and by a repeatable planning step, into a dependency-ordered backlog of whole-business work in which each step has a manager, a machine-checkable completion test, an independent review and an explicit point at which a person must act; a model asked to "run a business" without such structure produces unordered, unverifiable and non-resumable output.

[0443] Status of the chapter. Status: built (produced by agent sessions under the work-item ledger) for the function-chain process carried out by a project coordinator and agent sessions under the principal's direction, and for the artifacts that process produced. Status: prophetic (not yet built) for execution of the chain by the platform's own scheduled tick without a coordinating session, for the business function registry, artifact registry and dependency graph as database tables read by the tick, and for every automation described below that is not present in the repository. Status: built, partially operating for the console and the repository automation workflows named in section 58.

### 55.1 Function registry

[0444] In one embodiment the business function registry 3202 is a table `business_function` stored beside the work-item tables of section 3. Illustrative schema:

```
business_function(
  code            text primary key,      -- 'IP', 'FN', 'WEB', 'PR', 'BIZ', 'PL', 'GH', 'QA', 'PF'
  title           text not null,
  managing_role   text not null,         -- persona id, section 4
  artifact_schema text not null,         -- JSON: list of {kind, path_pattern, required, reviewer_role}
  done_check      text not null,         -- machine-checkable predicate over the artifact registry
  gate_class      text not null,         -- 'none' | 'filed' | 'identity' | 'filed+identity'
  default_milestone text not null,       -- 'Now' | 'Next' | 'After filing' | 'Later'
  generic_of      text                   -- fallback function code, see 57.4
)
```

[0445] "Gate class" has three base values. Class `none` means the function's artifacts may be produced and published without waiting for a person. Class `identity` means that finishing the function includes a step that is identity-bound under section 9 (a signature, a payment, an account only the principal can hold, an appearance in person). Class `filed` means the function's outputs would reveal matter whose protection depends on an earlier act (in the example of section 58, filing a provisional patent application), so the function's public outputs wait until a probe observes that act. Classes may be combined. The class is stored as data so that a tenant whose protective act is something other than a patent filing (a trademark application, a regulatory approval, an embargo date) changes the registry row and not the code.

[0446] The `artifact_schema` field makes "done" concrete. Example for function IP: `[{"kind":"disclosure","path_pattern":"ip/invention-disclosure.md","required":true,"reviewer_role":"reviewer"},{"kind":"prior_art_register","path_pattern":"ip/prior-art.md"},{"kind":"specification","path_pattern":"ip/provisional/*specification*.md"},{"kind":"figures","path_pattern":"ip/provisional/figures/*.svg"},{"kind":"claim_strategy","path_pattern":"ip/provisional/CLAIM-STRATEGY.md"},{"kind":"filing_checklist","path_pattern":"ip/provisional/FILING-CHECKLIST.md"},{"kind":"evidence_log","path_pattern":"ip/evidence-log.md"},{"kind":"deadline_calendar","path_pattern":null}]`. A null path pattern marks an artifact that lives outside the repository (a calendar entry) and is closed by a probe instead of a file test.

### 55.2 Artifact registry

[0447] The artifact registry 3204 is a table `artifact`:

```
artifact(
  id            text primary key,
  function_code text references business_function(code),
  item_id       text references items(id),   -- the work item that produced it
  kind          text, repo_path text not null,
  status        text check (status in ('planned','draft','reviewed','verified','stale','retired')),
  evidence      text,    -- commit hash, pull request, probe output
  reviewer      text,    -- persona id and model family of the reviewer
  content_hash  text,    -- hash of the file at the last review
  reviewed_at   text
)
```

[0448] Status transitions. An artifact enters `planned` when the planner creates its row, moves to `draft` when a staff persona commits a file at `repo_path`, moves to `reviewed` when a reviewer of a model family different from the author's family records a pass, and moves to `verified` when the function's done check (a predicate evaluated by the tick, for example "every required kind of the artifact schema is at least `reviewed` and the consistency check of section 57.2 returns an empty mismatch list") holds. An artifact moves to `stale` when the tick finds that `content_hash` no longer equals the hash of the file or when an artifact it depends on changed after the review. A stale artifact re-enters review and its function's done check fails until it is reviewed again. Technical effect: a downstream artifact (a pitch kit quoting a specification) cannot silently outlive a change to its source.

### 55.3 Dependency graph and gate labels

[0449] The dependency graph 3206 is a table `function_edge(from_code, to_code, kind)` with kinds `needs` (the target may start when the source's done check holds) and `gate` (the target may publish only when the source's gate probe holds). Example rows, in the form from -> to: `IP -> WEB (gate: filed)`, `IP -> PR (gate: filed)`, `BIZ -> FN (needs: entity chosen)`, `WEB -> FN (needs: live site, domain email)`, `IP -> QA (needs: draft)`, `QA -> IP (needs: mismatch list empty, for counsel review)`. The graph is required to be acyclic over `needs` edges; a validator run at write time rejects a cycle and writes a blocked work item naming it.

[0450] Ready test 3210. At each tick the manager selects from the backlog rows 3208 the highest-priority row for which: (a) every `needs` predecessor function has a done check that holds, or the row names a narrower trigger item that is done; (b) if the row carries a gate label (the label `gate:filed`), the gate probe of the matching `gate` edge holds; (c) no other claim is live on the item (section 13); and (d) the persona's work-in-progress limit permits it. A row failing (b) stays in the backlog under its milestone ("After filing") and is not an owner item, because nothing is asked of the principal for it; the dashboard of section 47 shows it as waiting on a named gate. When the gate probe later holds, the next tick treats the row as ready without any person moving it. Technical effect: the system refuses, by construction and without relying on an agent remembering a rule, to produce a public artifact that would defeat a protective filing.

[0451] Gate probes are ordinary probes of section 8. For the filing gate the probe may read a tracker field set by the principal's confirmation, check for a filing receipt in an owner-only repository, or query the patent office's public status interface where one is available. In one embodiment an unavailable probe is treated as "not held" so that the gate fails closed.

### 55.4 Backlog row schema

[0452] A backlog row 3208 is a work item (section 3) with additional columns, or a row in a view over the work item table: `owner_role`, `trigger` (a predecessor item or event), `done_check`, `milestone`, `labels` (for example `role:staff`, `ws:patent`, `gate:filed`), `function_code`, `identity_step` (a text naming what only the principal does, or null) and `prepared` (what the fleet prepares so that the principal's step is short). Example row, illustrative: item `FN-2`, principal "staff drafts, principal submits", trigger "BIZ-1, WEB-5", done check "submission confirmation", milestone "Next". An item whose `identity_step` is not null is created in two parts: the preparation part is executed by the fleet, and the identity-bound part is posted as an owner card (section 49) whose closing probe is the done check.

[0453] Alternatives: the registries may be YAML or JSON files validated by continuous integration; the graph may be derived from the `trigger` fields; gate classes may add `consent` for outreach (section 19, example 906b); a function may be owned by a pool with a lead that holds the claim.

## 56. Idea to function backlog: the bootstrap chain extended (FIG. 33)

[0454] FIG. 33 shows the chain from the idea file to a running function chain. Steps 3302 to 3314 are the bootstrap stages of section 19A (read, detect, select, plan, write, provision, start) and steps 3316 to 3326 are the repeated tick of the business functions.

[0455] Status: built, partially operating for steps 3302 to 3314 as implemented in the seed's bootstrapper: the read, detect, select, plan, write, provision and start stages exist as code and are tested offline with a dry run, and in the seed as it stands the plan stage emits the roles, the kit items KIT-0 to KIT-5 (and KIT-6 when the idea's constraints say nothing may be public), one outcome item per stated outcome and one scored prediction. Status: prophetic (not yet built) for a plan stage that also emits the function backlog described in 56.2 and writes the registries of section 55 as rows; in the repository the function backlog was produced by sessions (section 58). Status: prophetic for the first provisioning of a real cloud account: as of 2026-11-10 the provision and start stages have been exercised only in a dry run, and this disclosure does not state that they have been run against a real account.

### 56.1 Stages

[0456] Step 3302: the idea file states an idea, intended users, first outcomes and constraints. For a business the constraints may include a budget, a jurisdiction, a protective act (for example "nothing public until a patent application is filed") and a list of functions to include or exclude. Step 3304 reads the file, detects credential presence (never values) and tools, and opens an owner card for each missing credential with an `env:NAME` closing probe. Step 3306 selects a model tier from the budget and designates a reviewer model of a different family from the author model; the different-family rule is stored so the reviewer role cannot be given to the author's family by configuration drift (a validator rejects such a configuration). Step 3308 plans (56.2). Step 3310 writes the charter, role files, the registries of section 55, seed statements and the first artifacts' placeholders. Step 3312 provisions the database, worker and schedule. Step 3314 arms the schedule and records the heartbeat prediction of section 19A (six complete ticks in one hour in the example seed).

### 56.2 Planning step: turning an idea into the function backlog

[0457] The planner is a deterministic procedure with a model-assisted expansion. Inputs: the parsed idea, the business function registry 3202 (a default pack plus tenant rows), and the list of detected tools. Procedure:

```
1  functions = default_pack
2  for f in functions: if idea.excludes(f) or tools_missing_for(f) and f.requires_tool: mark f 'owner card'
3  for f in functions: instantiate f.artifact_schema -> artifact rows (status planned), paths filled from slug
4  for each (f, kind) in artifact rows: create backlog row {owner_role=f.managing_role,
       trigger=predecessor item from function_edge, done_check=f.done_check restricted to kind,
       milestone=f.default_milestone, labels=[role, workstream, 'gate:filed' if f.gate_class has 'filed']}
5  for each row with identity step: split into prepare item + owner card with closing probe
6  validate: acyclic needs edges; every row has responsible role, trigger, done check, milestone; every gate label has a gate edge
7  predictions: one per row with a measurable done check (item done by milestone date; artifact verified)
8  emit KIT items (charter, repos, runtime, heartbeat, reports, first plus, disclosure gate)
```

[0458] The model-assisted part is step 3: the planner may ask a model to expand an artifact schema for the particular idea (which prior-art queries, which funding programs fit, which events), and the expansion is stored as rows and checked by the validator of step 6, so that a model's output cannot introduce a row without an responsible role, trigger, done check and milestone. Worked example, illustrative: an idea of an autonomous development platform with a constraint "nothing public until filed" produces backlog rows in the milestones Now, Next, After filing and Later; seven rows carry `gate:filed` (the section 58 example has six) and the KIT item "hold the disclosure gate" is emitted because the constraint text contains the phrase. If the idea instead states no protective act, no row carries a gate label and the marketing and web rows are ready at once. Alternatives: the planner may be a table of templates per industry (software, media, services, research, nonprofit) with no model; it may be run again on a changed idea file, in which case it creates only rows that do not yet exist and proposes retirement of rows whose function was excluded.

### 56.3 The tick for business functions

[0459] Steps 3316 to 3326 are the manager tick of section 5 applied to the function backlog. Step 3316 takes the next ready item by the ready test 3210. Step 3318 records a prediction (section 8), for example "artifact status verified by the milestone date", then runs the executor of the persona to produce the artifact in the repository by a pull request through the change path of section 12. Step 3320 submits the artifact to review (section 57.1) and runs the consistency check (section 57.2). Step 3322 asks whether the next step is both irreversible and identity-bound; if so the system first tries to construct a reversible form (for example a submission package with an unsent draft, or a form filled in up to the signature) and, where none exists, step 3324 posts an owner card carrying the prepared package, the default in effect and a closing probe. Step 3326 closes the item with evidence, updates the trust ledger for the (surface, action class) pair, sets the artifact status, and proposes a plus.

[0460] All business functions run under the same rules as development work: the tick of section 24, predictions scored as in section 8, trust accrued per (surface, action class) as in section 9, the plus share of section 7 and the interrupt gate of section 9. Each function's surface is its own: the repository surface for documents, the work tracker for items, the site surface for pages and a funder's portal surface for submissions. A new surface starts at the lowest rung, so a function that has never published a page cannot publish one unreviewed merely because development work has earned a high rung on the repository.

### 56.4 Function managers

[0461] In one embodiment the personas of section 4 are extended by function: a business manager owns the plan and cross-function priorities; a portfolio manager owns capacity allocation and the disclosure gate; project managers deliver a backlog; a product manager owns the roadmap, pricing hypotheses and the plus queue; a marketing and promotion manager owns story, site, content calendar and case study; a sales and partnerships manager owns the lead list and partner programs; a funding manager owns the funding map and an application kit per credit or grant program (not accounting, payments or bookkeeping; see 57.6); a legal and IP manager owns the patent timeline, evidence log, trademark and contract templates; customer success owns onboarding; operations owns accounts, deploys, domains and mail; research staff own competitive intelligence, prior art and event scouting; and a reviewer and QA persona independent of all of them checks outputs. Each has an owner area, a trigger that wakes it, a done check and a four-line report as in section 50. A table row for each persona states "what only the principal does" (for example: signs filings, moves money, approves publication under the principal's name, appears in person), which feeds the `identity_step` column of 55.4. Status: built (produced by agent sessions under the ledger; the roster is stored as a document, section 58). Status: prophetic for running these personas as role rows on the platform's scheduled tick.

## 57. Review, consistency checks, function artifacts and fallbacks

### 57.1 Review by a different-family reviewer

[0462] Every artifact of a function whose schema names a `reviewer_role` passes review before its status can exceed `draft`. The reviewer is a model of a family different from the model that wrote the artifact (section 36, two-family review). The reviewer receives the artifact, the artifacts it cites and the function's done check, and returns a verdict with quotations of the passages relied on (so that a pass is not a bare assertion) and a list of defects, each with a severity. A reviewer may not review its own output; the registry records the reviewer persona and family, and a validator refuses a `reviewed` status where the families are equal. A defect of severity at least medium reopens the item with the defect as its next action. A pass is recorded against `content_hash`, so a later change makes the artifact `stale`. Technical effect: independent review catches shared-blind-spot errors that same-family self-review misses, and the content hash prevents a review from being reused for text it did not see.

### 57.2 Consistency check across artifacts

[0463] A function of the chain can produce several artifacts that must say the same thing (for the IP function: the invention disclosure, the specification, the figure list, the claim summaries, the public documentation and the pitch material). The consistency check is a work item of class QA that: (a) extracts a list of load-bearing statements from each artifact (defined terms, numerals, counts, status labels, dates, thresholds, named components); (b) aligns them by key; (c) emits a mismatch list with one row per pair of artifacts that disagree, per statement present in one and absent from another where the function schema requires it, and per statement whose status label is inconsistent (for example one artifact calls a mechanism built and another prophetic); and (d) passes only when the mismatch list is empty. In one embodiment the done check of the QA item is literally "mismatch list empty". A mismatch opens a work item on the artifact designated as the source of truth by the function schema, and the other artifacts are marked `stale` until the source is changed or the mismatch is dispositioned as intended. Alternatives: the extraction may be a model call whose output is stored as data and re-checked by pattern tests; the alignment may be by embedding similarity above a threshold with a human-readable explanation; for numbers the check may be exact string comparison after normalization.

[0464] Public-text check. For a function with gate class `filed`, a second check scans the organization's public surfaces for mechanism terms listed in a deny list and fails when one appears before the gate probe holds (in one implementation a console worker fetches a configured page and fails when a listed mechanism word appears; section 58). The check is the machine form of a stored rule, so an agent that does not remember the rule is still stopped.

### 57.3 Function artifacts

[0465] Intellectual property function. Artifacts: invention disclosure; prior-art register with one row per reference, what it teaches, how the disclosure differs and which claim families carry the difference (with a stated limit such as "non-patent literature only; a professional patent search is still required"); provisional specification with figures and claim-style summaries; claim strategy memo; filing checklist; evidence log of dated conception and reduction-to-practice records; a deadline calendar entry for the conversion deadline with reminders at 90, 60 and 30 days before twelve months from filing. Filing is identity-bound. The system prepares a filing-ready package (specification as a portable document, drawings document, checklist of forms, fee and entity-status note, a list of the questions for counsel) and posts a gate card to the principal. The card states what is asked (review and file), why it is gated (the filing needs the principal's identity and signature and cannot be undone), the default in effect (nothing is published), what the fleet already prepared, and a closing probe that reads the filing receipt. The system does not sign, pay or submit. Inventorship requires natural persons, so the filing checklist records the principal's conception contributions for counsel.

[0466] Marketing function. Artifacts: customer segments with a beachhead and a first-customer profile; a competition landscape with dated sources; positioning and category; a pitch kit with a one-line, a 30-second and a two-minute pitch and objection answers; a promotion plan by audience and channel; a content calendar. Every statement carries a disclosure tag: SAFE NOW (an outcome-level statement that may be said while a protective act is pending), AFTER FILING (names a mechanism and waits for the gate) or NDA ONLY (numbers, schemas, claim language). Tags are data, and the public-text check of 57.2 reads them. Funding function: a funding map (non-dilutive first), a kit per program with eligibility, gate, credit ask, use of credits mapped to milestones and a submit-ready SAFE NOW answer set separate from the internal full version; submission is identity-bound. Events function: a dated calendar of conferences, demo days and deadlines each tagged before or after the gate, with estimates marked "est." Customer research function: discovery-call scripts using only SAFE NOW language and a log of conversations. Branding function: a brand kit (name rules, tagline, voice, palette, type, logo files, templates), public organization profile contents, a site and a dashboard. Legal and entity function: a comparison note and owner cards for entity choice, assignment of rights, trademark clearance, contract terms and tax elections, each marked for a professional; the system prepares and does not decide. Web presence: a site deployed behind access control and noindex until the gate opens, a post-gate step list (attach domains, remove noindex, change the footer to a patent-pending statement, search-console verification) and a dashboard.

### 57.4 Generic-over-domain fallbacks

[0467] The registry's `generic_of` column implements fallbacks. When the tenant's idea names a function that has no registry row, the planner selects the nearest generic function by (a) tenant-supplied mapping, (b) a stored default pack of generic functions (research, plan, build, review, publish, fund, protect, report) and (c) as a last resort the generic function "research then plan then build then review", whose artifact schema is a single document and whose gate class is `identity` if the idea names any step requiring signature, payment or an account. Industries: the default pack is parameterized by industry template (software, media or marketing, operations or finance, research laboratory, nonprofit as in section 19) that swap function titles, artifact kinds and probes without code change. Platforms: the repository surface may be any version-control host, the tracker any issue system, the runtime any scheduled-execution service (section 46), and the publication target any static-site or edge host. Executors: any executor of section 38 may produce an artifact; the artifact registry stores which, and the trust ledger is kept per executor. When a required platform is absent (no tracker), the work item table of section 3 is the tracker and the function backlog is exported as files.

### 57.5 Self-directed bootstrapping with light collaborative input

[0468] "Collaborative input" means any message, edit, answer or file that the principal supplies at any time, in any channel the console of section 47 accepts, without it being a precondition of progress. The system manages itself: the planner of 56.2 runs on the idea file alone, and the tick of 56.3 proceeds on every ready item without waiting for input. Input is handled by four rules. (a) Absorption: each input is stored as a record with a timestamp and classified by the same recognizer as other signals (section 23) into a direction change (the planner re-runs on the amended idea and creates, retires or reprioritizes rows), an answer to an open question (applied through the answers table of section 49.4), a correction of an artifact (opens a work item against the artifact and marks dependents stale), or a comment (stored, no action). (b) Defaults: every open question has a default in effect, so unanswered questions do not block. (c) Cards only for identity-bound steps: an owner card is created only when step 3322 finds an irreversible, identity-bound step with no constructible reversible form; a question that merely improves quality is answered by the fleet's inference from the record and is shown in the "fleet is handling" list. (d) Burden cap: the number of open owner cards is capped (for example three shown at once, ranked by severity and nearness of deadline) and each card is closable with one tap. Technical effect: the principal's attention is spent only on steps that need the principal's identity, and a person who gives no input for a period does not stop the chain. Status: built (produced by agent sessions under the ledger) for a short list of principal items in a roadmap source document and the owner queue of the console (sections 47 to 49); prophetic for classification of arbitrary input into the four classes by the platform's own tick.

### 57.6 Provisional patent preparation by a non-professional

[0469] For a principal who is not a patent professional, the IP function of 57.3 is run so that the principal's part is review and filing. The system (a) interviews from the repository record rather than from a questionnaire (the disclosure is drafted from design documents, code and dated history); (b) drafts the specification with sections for definitions, system overview, data model, mechanisms with steps and alternatives, worked examples, status sentences that separate built from prophetic embodiments, figures drawn as line art with unique reference numerals, a brief description of the drawings and claim-style summaries; (c) drafts a claim strategy memo kept out of the filed text, listing independent families, the technical effect each argues and the nearest known art with the gap; (d) drafts a filing checklist (documents, forms, entity status and fee options, inventorship note, a list of questions for counsel, and the clock questions on prior public disclosure expressed as questions); (e) drafts a prior-art register and states its limit; (f) writes the evidence log from dated history; (g) posts one gate card to the principal; and (h) after filing, schedules the conversion deadline reminders. The principal or an attorney files; the system does not file, and the output is a draft for attorney review and not legal advice, which the artifacts state on their first page. Status: built (produced by agent sessions under the ledger, section 58.2); prophetic for generation of this package by the platform's own tick from an idea file for a tenant other than the first.

### 57.7 Search and analytics integration in one platform

[0470] Services that each do one part (a site builder, an analytics product, a search console, an SEO tool, a task tracker) leave the principal to carry facts between them. In the described platform the web-presence function (57.3) spans the chain in one data model. The steps are: deploy the site behind access control and noindex (gate class `filed`); after the gate probe holds, attach domains, remove noindex, change the footer, verify site ownership with a search console and submit a sitemap, then enable analytics. Steps that need the principal's account (granting a service account access to a search-console property, or enabling an interface in the principal's cloud project) are owner cards with closing probes that observe the grant. Ingest workers then read search and visit data daily, for one complete day with a stated lag, into a signal store as rows `(date, source, channel, entity_type, entity_id, metric, value, quality)`; when a credential is missing or refused the worker writes a ledger row with the reason and no number, so that a metric reads "not connected" rather than zero. Loops act on the signals: a page with impressions and low click-through opens a title-and-description work item for the marketing manager; a query with impressions and no page opens a content item; a drop in sessions after a deploy opens an incident item through the probe of section 42; a landing page whose bounce rises over its baseline lowers the plus score of the change that touched it. Each loop produces a prediction before acting and is scored after (section 8). Status: built in one implementation of a console worker, which runs daily ingest jobs that read analytics and search-console data into a signal table, with a per-source "not connected" state and an owner card for the service-account grant; prophetic for site verification and sitemap submission as automated steps, for the loops that act on the signals, and for any of this running for the product's own site, where the post-gate steps exist as backlog rows labelled with the gate label.

### 57.8 Scope exclusion: financial back-end

[0471] Financial back-end functions, namely accounting, bookkeeping, payment processing, invoicing, payroll, tax filing and treasury, are not part of the described embodiments, and nothing in this chapter describes or is intended to describe them. The funding function of 57.3 prepares applications and kits for credit and grant programs and posts owner cards for submission; money movement, signing and certification remain with the principal and with professionals.

## 58. Worked example: a business function chain for a product organization (FIG. 32, FIG. 33)

[0472] This section describes a worked example of the function chain of sections 55 to 57 for a product organization, so that the chain is anchored in an artifact set. It also states what has not been done. "Produced by agent sessions" means that the artifacts were written by hosted coding-agent sessions coordinated by a project coordinator, under direction from the principal and the work-item ledger, and merged through reviewed changes; it does not mean that the platform's own scheduled tick generated them.

[0473] Status: built (produced by agent sessions under the ledger) for the function-chain process and the artifacts. Status: prophetic (not yet built) for unattended end-to-end execution by the platform's own tick, and for any automation not described as built. The platform has not autonomously run a business, and its own scheduled loops have not yet run the chain end to end; this section does not state otherwise.

### 58.1 The idea and the backlog

[0474] In the example the backlog is held as a roadmap source document, an exported roadmap, project definitions and a set of about forty issue files, with function-prefixed rows for intellectual property, funding, business planning, web presence, repository organization, platform, promotion, performance and quality assurance, plus a label file listing about seventeen labels including the gate label `gate:filed`, role labels and workstream labels. Each row states a responsible role, a trigger, a done check and a status; for example the counsel-review row has the principal as responsible role, the draft and the quality-assurance row as trigger, and "review notes resolved" as done check; the quality-assurance row has the reviewer as responsible role, the draft as trigger and "mismatch list empty" as done check; a funding-submission row reads "staff drafts, the principal submits". The milestones are Now, Next, After filing and Later. Six rows carry the gate label: a pitch deck, making an organization profile externally visible, trademark clearance, a case study, attaching domains and changing the site footer, and search-console verification. The roadmap states the capacity rule that 20 percent of each cycle goes to accepted pluses. Rows 3208 of FIG. 32 therefore exist as built data in file form (not as database rows read by a tick).

### 58.2 Artifacts by function

[0475] Business planning: a set of eight numbered documents (a business manager's verdict, offer and plan with risks; a management roster of twelve virtual managers and staff with owned area, wake trigger, done check and what only the principal does, in a table; customers; competition; a pitch kit; funding; promotion; and a dated events calendar with before-or-after-filing tags). A cover document sets the three disclosure tags SAFE NOW, AFTER FILING and NDA ONLY, marks professional-review items ([LAWYER], [ACCOUNTANT]) and includes a review table with every file at status "draft". Funding and credits: an application kit, a deck outline and a one-pager. Marketing: positioning, audiences and channels. Branding: a brand kit (name, tagline, voice, a rule on what may be externally visible, logo files in vector and raster form, profile text, social templates, document and e-mail templates and a render tool). Intellectual property: an invention disclosure, a prior-art register (with the limit that only non-patent literature was searched and that counsel must search patent databases), an evidence log, a bootstrapping-embodiment note, a provisional package with a specification, figures as vector files, a drawings document, a claim strategy memo and a filing checklist, and register documents. Web presence and dashboard: a site worker (starter page, a declaration of deploy probes, a test) and a dashboard worker (SQL schema, seed, test) implementing the console of sections 47 to 49 including a disclosure guard. Repository automation: five workflows (checks, index, issues, lane, organization management) with three scripts and an automation description of the issue-to-merge loop and its clocks (priority service levels of 4 hours, 24 hours, 72 hours and 7 days; lane every 10 minutes; hourly triage).

[0476] Status for 58.2: built (produced by agent sessions under the ledger). Status: built, partially operating for the workflows, which exist as files; unattended merging by the lane is described but is not asserted to have run to completion. Status: built, partially operating for the dashboard and site, which exist as code with tests; their deployed state is not asserted.

### 58.3 Gates, principal steps and review in the example

[0477] Filing, entity choice, account creation, credit and grant submissions, signing and payment are principal steps in the roster (seven categories of what only the principal does) and appear as rows owned by the principal (counsel review, filing, entity, repositories, funding submissions). A short list in the roadmap source ranks the principal's nine items with an estimated time and "what the fleet prepares". The filing checklist records the micro and small entity routes, the documents to upload and the open questions for counsel (timing questions are stated as questions for counsel and not as conclusions). Marketing waits for filing as in FIG. 32: the rows for the pitch deck, case study, domains and footer, search-console verification, trademark clearance and profile carry the gate label, and the events document states that talks, demos and booths wait while private accelerator and grant applications may go in with SAFE NOW wording. The independent-review row ("mismatch list empty") has the draft as trigger and is a Now row; its status in the issue file is "open", and this chapter does not state that a mismatch list was produced and found empty. The intellectual-property row's status reads "draft done" for the specification, figures, drawings and checklist.

### 58.4 What has not been run

[0478] The following are prophetic. (a) A tick that reads the registries of section 55 as database tables and chooses the next function item; in the example the roadmap is files and issues. (b) The business function registry, artifact registry and dependency graph as tables; the existing equivalents are the roadmap's responsible-role, trigger, done check and label columns, the issue files and prose planning documents. (c) A plan stage of the seed's bootstrapper that emits the function backlog; the seed emits kit items KIT-0 to KIT-6 and outcome items. (d) The consistency check of 57.2 as a scheduled job; the quality-assurance item exists as a backlog row. (e) Unattended review by a different-family model of each artifact with recorded verdicts and content hashes. (f) The first cloud provisioning of an organization by the seed. (g) The platform's own scheduled loops running the chain, producing the artifacts and posting the principal's cards, end to end.

[0479] The roster in the example also lists a finance manager whose area includes budget and runway; those financial functions are outside the described embodiments (57.8) and are not relied on for any claim.

### 58.5 Relation to the self-description of chapter H

[0480] The registries of section 55 are themselves documents that the organization describes about itself. Chapter H describes a self-description mechanism in which the organization's own records (registries, boundary file, probes, instance description) are read by the platform to answer what it is, what it has and what it does. In one embodiment the business function registry 3202, artifact registry 3204 and dependency graph 3206 are among the records so described: the self-description lists each function, its artifacts and their verified status, and the public-text check of 57.2 reads the same registry to know which statements are held by the gate. A disagreement between the self-description and the artifact registry (for example a function listed as verified whose artifact is stale) is an input to the consistency check and opens a work item. The reference to chapter H is to its mechanism as described there and adds no further embodiment here.

## 59. Evidence index (non-limiting)

[0481] The following categories of evidence of built embodiments exist in the applicant's records. They are listed generically, they do not limit the disclosure, and they are not a statement about any audience or date of use. (a) A versioned governing-policy document with a lineage table of its amendments and a mapped implementation of its registries. (b) Database migrations that create the ledgers, views and triggers described. (c) Scheduled-run records with per-step rows at ten-minute and hourly cadences. (d) Predictions and outcomes with scored match counts. (e) Failure-mode detectors and remediation-contract rows. (f) Audit documents of a worker fleet and their correction documents. (g) A seed code base with unit tests and a dry-run bootstrap. (h) Console worker code with automated tests of its authentication, queue and command behavior. (i) Repository history of agent-produced artifacts for the business function chain, and a draft provisional package. (j) Boundary-file check output and product-claim probe rows.

# CLAIM-STYLE SUMMARIES

[0482] Claims are not required in a provisional application. The following numbered statements describe aspects for which protection may be sought in a later application; they do not limit the disclosure. They are organized in families. Each family has one independent method, one independent system and one independent non-transitory medium, followed by dependent statements ordered from broader to narrower, so that a later application may fold a narrower statement into its parent. As used below, a "surface" is a system or account that an agent process can reach through a credential or a binding; an "action class" is a category of operation on a surface; a "gate card" is a record in a queue of a principal that states a step, a reason, a default in effect and a closing probe; a "closing probe" is a read-only query whose passing result shows that a step is done; a "tick" is one execution of a repeating procedure of ordered steps; and a "work item" is a record in a work ledger; and a "principal" is a person on whose behalf an autonomous system operates. Statements that rest on text of the description labelled prophetic appear only as dependent statements. Financial back-end functions (accounting, bookkeeping, payment processing, invoicing, payroll, tax filing, treasury) are not claimed.

**Family 1: management hierarchy, allocation and product improvement**

1. A computer-implemented method comprising: storing, in a database accessible to a plurality of agent processes executing on one or more processors, persona records, each persona record associating an agent process with a role in a management hierarchy comprising a portfolio-manager role and a plurality of program-manager roles, with a program, with at least one metric, and with an enumerated set of permitted operations on work items; on each of a plurality of scheduled ticks: executing, as the agent process associated with the portfolio-manager role, a procedure that computes a risk value for each program from records of open work items and metric readings, writes the risk values once per tick to a snapshot table, and writes allocation records each assigning to a program a number of focus slots from a bounded total, at least part of the bounded total being assigned in proportion to the risk values; reading the snapshot table, in steps of the tick that follow the writing, instead of re-evaluating a database view from which the risk values are derived; executing, for each program, the agent process associated with a program-manager role of the program, which ranks open work items of the program in an order in which work items associated with the at least one metric of the program precede other work items, selects ranked work items not exceeding the number of focus slots of the program, and writes for each selected work item a priority record, identifying the agent process that wrote it, into a scoring table; computing, by an execution queue, a dispatch order of open work items using the scoring table, and dispatching work items in the dispatch order to executor processes that generate changes to a managed computing system and deploy the changes to the managed computing system; recording, for each operation performed by an agent process on a work item, an action record comprising the work item, the operation, a prior value and a new value; and denying, by a tool layer that determines an identity of a requesting agent process, a request to perform an operation that is not enumerated in the persona record associated with the identity.

2. A system comprising one or more processors and a memory storing instructions that, when executed by the one or more processors, cause the system to perform the method of claim 1.

3. A non-transitory computer-readable medium storing instructions that, when executed by one or more processors, cause the one or more processors to perform the method of claim 1.

4. The method of claim 1, wherein the risk value is a weighted sum of at least a count of open work items having a priority label of high or critical, a count of open work items, a count of breaches of guard metrics, and a count of stalled projects, a stalled project being a project that has open work items, no work item that changed state within a stated number of days, and no live claim.

5. The method of claim 1, wherein a first part of the bounded total is divided evenly among programs having open work items and a second part is divided in proportion to each program's share of a sum of the risk values, and wherein a program whose risk value rose by more than a rise threshold within a window receives additional focus slots.

6. The method of claim 1, wherein the ranking orders work items associated with a primary metric of the program first, work items associated with a guard metric of the program second, and remaining work items by priority label and then by age.

7. The method of claim 1, wherein each agent process associated with a program-manager role deletes only priority records it wrote and inserts priority records without overwriting priority records written by another agent process.

8. The method of claim 1, wherein the operations comprise closing a work item filed by a metric trigger when a reading of the metric is on target, the reading was refreshed within a refresh window, the work item is older than a minimum age, and no remediation contract of the work item is failing, the reading being stored as closing evidence, and wherein the metric trigger files the work item again upon a later breach.

9. The method of claim 1, wherein the operations comprise lowering a priority label of a work item from critical to high when the work item was filed by an agent process more than twenty-four hours earlier, has no doer line and no linked session, was not filed by a metric trigger and is not a focus item of a primary or guard metric, a doer line being a target file and an anchor text written as first lines of the work item, and wherein work items filed by a principal or by a session are excluded from lowering.

10. The method of claim 1, further comprising, for a selected work item that has lacked a doer line for a stated period, requesting from a model-backed agent a target file and an anchor text, validating that the target file is outside excluded paths and that the anchor text occurs exactly once in the target file, and either writing the target file and the anchor text into the work item as a doer line or recording a no-fix finding with a reason.

11. The method of claim 1, wherein the dispatch order is computed from a score that is a product of an impact, a probability, a sum of a capability gain and a reach gain, an inverse of a remediation cost, a trust score of the surface and action class to be used by the work item, and one plus a confidence of an inferred-direction record for a category of the work item.

12. The method of claim 1, further comprising: maintaining a product register separate from a program register, each product record comprising a usage measure, a quality measure, a last-improvement time and a state; reserving for product improvement a share of a bounded change capacity that is not reallocated to repair work items when a risk value rises; selecting a product by at least its last-improvement time, subject to at most one improvement in flight per product; generating, by a model-backed agent from evidence read from the managed computing system, a single additive change to the selected product and a prediction of a direction and a size of change of an outcome measure; dispatching the additive change as a work item through the dispatching to the executor processes; upon verified deployment, storing a baseline of the outcome measure and of a guard measure; and, after a measurement window, reading both measures and marking the additive change kept when the outcome measure improved by at least a materiality threshold and the guard measure did not worsen by more than a tolerance, and otherwise filing a work item that reverts the additive change, a missing reading, or a reading older than a staleness limit, being treated as not improved.

13. The method of claim 12, wherein a kept additive change causes the next additive change generated for the same product to be seeded with a reference to the kept additive change.

14. The method of claim 12, wherein the usage measure is copied from a request-analytics snapshot of a platform and is stored as null, and not as zero, when the snapshot is older than the staleness limit or missing.

15. The method of claim 12, wherein the additive change is a treatment of an experiment having a hypothesis, a control, an outcome metric and a window, the method further comprising reading counts for the treatment and the control over identical calendar windows, stopping the experiment at the earliest of a close of the window, a breach of a guard metric and a futility bound, and promoting the treatment to standard operating procedure when it shows a positive delta on at least two metrics and otherwise dropping the treatment with a written rationale.

16. The method of claim 1, further comprising: storing work items each having an identifier, an owner role, at least one trigger identifier and a done check; placing a work item in a blocked state recording a blocking identifier when a trigger identifier does not name a work item in a done state, and in a ready state otherwise; placing a ready work item in an in-progress state on a claim by a doer in the owner role, the claim being released after an idle expiry; handing a work item asserted done to a reviewer role distinct from the doer; executing the closing probe named in the done check, placing the work item in the done state with stored evidence on a pass and returning it to the in-progress state on a failure; and, on entry of a work item to the done state, re-evaluating each work item that names the identifier of the entered work item as a trigger identifier.

17. The method of claim 1, further comprising: reading ratified objectives from an objectives store that is writable only by a ratification action of the principal; generating, by an agent process, candidate goal statements each having a falsifiable definition of done; classifying each candidate goal statement as an instrumental goal or as an objective revision; writing each objective revision with a proposed status into a ratification queue without adopting it; and, for each instrumental goal, computing a score, rejecting the instrumental goal when the score is below a score threshold or when a similarity of the instrumental goal to a goal adopted within a window is at or above a similarity threshold, and otherwise adopting the instrumental goal as a derived goal recorded with a parent objective among the ratified objectives, an alignment statement and the definition of done, and writing the derived goal to a ledger read by a scheduled consumer.

18. The method of claim 17, wherein the similarity is the greater of a Jaccard similarity and a containment similarity of token sets of the instrumental goal and the goal adopted within the window.

19. The method of claim 17, wherein a mutation request to the objectives store is executed under an authentication check, and an attempt to give an objective revision an adopted status is converted to the proposed status.

20. The method of claim 17, further comprising, for each issue category in which the principal filed at least a minimum number of work items within a window, writing an inferred-direction record with a confidence and multiplying a queue score of work items in the category by one plus the confidence, wherein the inferred-direction record does not alter the ratified objectives or any authority limit.

**Family 2: earned autonomy, trust ladder and autonomy policy as data**

21. A computer-implemented method comprising: storing, in a policy store, for each of a plurality of surfaces a permission ceiling and, for each action class of the surface, a reversible flag and an identity-bound flag; validating the policy store, the validating rejecting an action class that is irreversible and not identity-bound and that has neither a recorded reversible path nor a denial; maintaining a trust ledger keyed by surface and action class, each entry comprising counts of verified successes and of failures and a trust score computed from the counts and a prior; mapping each trust score to one rung of an ordered autonomy ladder, the rung not exceeding the permission ceiling of the surface, and storing a transition record upon each change of rung; upon receiving a proposed action by an agent process, determining a surface and an action class of the proposed action and selecting an execution path according to the rung of the surface and action class, wherein at least one lower rung requires an added review step before execution and at least one higher rung permits execution without the added review step; when the proposed action remains irreversible and identity-bound after an attempt to construct a reversible form of the proposed action, refraining from executing the proposed action, writing a gate card comprising a default in effect, a description of what has been prepared and a closing probe, continuing other work, and closing the gate card automatically when the closing probe observes a step of the principal; otherwise executing the proposed action through the selected execution path, verifying a result of the proposed action with at least one probe, and updating the counts; listing, in a deny list of harness settings of an agent product, each shell-command pattern that the policy store lists for an action class held by the gate card, so that a tool call of the agent product matching the pattern is denied, and failing a consistency test when such a pattern is absent from the deny list.

23. A non-transitory computer-readable medium storing instructions that, when executed by one or more processors, cause the one or more processors to perform the method of claim 21.

24. The method of claim 21, wherein the trust score equals (S + a) divided by (S + F + a + b), where S is the count of verified successes, F is the count of failures and a and b are positive constants.

25. The method of claim 24, wherein a and b are each 5, and the ordered autonomy ladder has seven rungs, named observed, assisted, delegated, autonomous, self-directed, self-modifying and self-extending, whose lower bounds on the trust score are 0, 0.3, 0.5, 0.6, 0.7, 0.8 and 0.9 respectively.

The method of claim 21, wherein the rung changes only by a reviewed change to the policy store and not by a tick procedure, a rung being promoted when a count of outcomes meets a minimum and a reliability meets a promotion threshold with no severe failure, and being demoted when the reliability falls below a demotion threshold or upon a hidden failure, a silent drop or an attempt to bypass the gate card.

27. The method of claim 21, wherein no rung of the ordered autonomy ladder permits a change to a harness permission check, a secret store or a classification rule of the gate card, and wherein at a rung above the rung that permits execution without the added review step the agent processes modify their own tick procedures or policy only through a reviewed, versioned and revertible change.

The method of claim 21, further comprising constructing the reversible form of the proposed action by at least one of: taking a backup and verifying that the backup is readable; staging the proposed action behind a flag that defaults to off; deploying the proposed action to a canary; writing a draft instead of sending; and splitting the proposed action into a reversible part executed immediately and an irreversible part, and executing without a request to the principal each action that is or has been made reversible.

29. The method of claim 21, further comprising storing, for a decision of an agent process, a prediction record comprising a subject, a comparison operator, a value and a due time, and, at or after the due time, reading the subject from a managed computing system, storing an observed value and a matched indicator, and counting the matched indicator in the trust ledger.

30. The method of claim 21, wherein a card lacking an identity-bound line is rerouted to the agent processes instead of the principal.

31. The method of claim 21, further comprising multiplying a priority of a work item by the trust score of the surface and action class that the work item will use.

32. The method of claim 21, wherein the trust score decays toward the prior for each period in which the surface and action class are not exercised, and is changed by weights associated with trust events, the weights for verified autonomous actions and clean self-remediations increasing the trust score and the weights for questions to the principal, handoffs of an executable step to the principal, silent drops and hidden failures decreasing the trust score, the weight of a hidden failure exceeding each increasing weight.

33. The method of claim 32, wherein the trust score starts at 0.5, the decay is 0.01 per unexercised period, the weight of a hidden failure is 0.30, and the trust score is clamped to an interval from 0 to 1.

The method of claim 21, further comprising, before each model call and each tool dispatch of an agent process, requesting a fresh state from a control authority over a service binding whose caller identity is a trusted property of the binding, permitting the dispatch only on a valid not-paused response, and applying a resume by compare-and-set on a revision.

35. A computer-implemented method comprising: storing, in a database accessible to one or more agent processes executing on one or more processors, a register of recognitions, each recognition comprising a signature of a draft output pattern and a redirect, the draft output patterns including at least a conditional offer to act, a question directed to a principal, a menu of options for the principal and a sentence naming the principal as the actor of a next action; for each draft output of an agent process, matching the draft output against the signatures; for each match, classifying the match as an excluded match when the draft output states a refusal or limit together with a reason, when a stop stated in the draft output is irreversible and identity-bound and no reversible form of the stop is constructible, or when a step named in the draft output is held by a live claim of another agent process, and otherwise classifying the match as a false stop; for each false stop, causing the action abandoned by the draft output to be executed through a change path that deploys a change to a managed computing system, and verifying a result of the action; writing a hit record for each match; and reducing, by a weight associated with the matched signature, a trust value associated with a surface and an action class of the agent process.

46.
