**Family 10: self-knowledge records and self-description**

116. A computer-implemented method comprising: storing, in a database, a plurality of beliefs of an autonomous system about itself or its environment, each belief having a falsifying probe that is a read-only query whose result contradicts the belief when the probe fails; executing each probe through a probe runner that refuses any statement that is not a single read-only query; when a probe returns a result other than a passing result, marking the belief as falsified with a time and opening a work item through a remediation contract; storing an autonomy policy stating, for each of a plurality of surfaces, a permission ceiling and a rung; and, by a manager tick, calling a policy lookup that returns, for an action on a surface, one of allow, propose only and deny from the autonomy policy, and, on deny, recording a reason, taking a next work item and not retrying the action or a reworded form of the action.

117. A system comprising one or more processors and a memory storing instructions that, when executed by the one or more processors, cause the system to perform the method of claim 116.

118. A non-transitory computer-readable medium storing instructions that, when executed by one or more processors, cause the one or more processors to perform the method of claim 116.

119. The method of claim 116, wherein a belief marked falsified is rendered as falsified in a self-description of the autonomous system until a later execution of its probe passes, and a probe that errors is rendered as unverified and not as passing.

120. The method of claim 116, further comprising storing an access map comprising, for each surface, a name of a credential or binding that grants access and a boolean presence value determined by testing whether the credential or binding is set, wherein a field of the access map holding a value other than a boolean is refused.

121. The method of claim 119, further comprising storing an honest-gap list whose entries each have a closing probe, and rendering a capability that lacks an evidence reference in the honest-gap list as described and not exercised.

122. The method of claim 121, further comprising assigning each statement of the self-description a class selected from measured, structural, observed limit and prophetic, the evidence for a measured statement being a probe result within a freshness limit, the evidence for a structural statement being a result of a deterministic check with a commit identifier, the evidence for an observed-limit statement being a recorded refusal or failure, and a prophetic statement carrying a label, an owner and a trigger.

123. The method of claim 122, further comprising: rendering the self-description in a canonical order so that unchanged state yields identical text; computing a difference between the rendered self-description and a committed copy stored in a repository; applying a consistency check that fails for a statement lacking the evidence required by its class and, on failure, moving the statement to the honest-gap list or opening a work item carrying a closing probe without deleting the statement silently; and, when the difference is non-empty, opening a change to the committed copy that is reviewed by an agent other than the generator, and appending a lineage row on merge.

124. The method of claim 123, wherein the consistency check further fails when a permission ceiling or rung embedded in the self-description differs from the autonomy policy, when a prophetic statement is rendered as a present capability, or when the access map holds a non-boolean value, so that the self-description cannot widen authority.

125. The method of claim 123, further comprising, when the difference arises from an edit to the committed copy that does not match the stored records, opening a work item reporting the edit and not overwriting the edit.

126. The method of claim 123, further comprising loading the self-description by a new session that holds no prior memory, deriving permission ceilings, rungs, standing limits, falsified beliefs and gaps, and re-running a probe on which the session relies when a freshness stamp of the probe is older than a limit.

**Family 11: autonomous business function chain from an idea file**

127. A computer-implemented method comprising: reading an idea file stating an idea, intended users, outcomes and constraints; generating, from the idea file and a set of business functions each having a managing persona, an artifact schema, a done check and a gate class, a backlog of work items, each work item having a function code, an owner role, a trigger naming a predecessor work item or event, a done check, a milestone and, for a work item of a business function whose gate class is a filing gate class, a gate label; validating that the triggers contain no cycle and that each work item has an owner role, a trigger, a done check and a milestone; selecting, by a coordinating process, a work item as ready only when each predecessor named by its trigger is done, a gate probe matching the gate label of the work item, if any, holds, and no claim is live on the work item; producing, by an executor of the managing persona, an artifact at a repository path through a change path, and recording the artifact in an artifact record with a status; submitting the artifact to a reviewer model of a model family different from the model family that produced the artifact, and recording a verdict against a hash of the artifact; setting the status to verified only when the done check holds and a consistency check across a plurality of artifacts of the business function returns an empty mismatch list; and scanning public surfaces for terms of a deny list and failing the scan when a term appears before the gate probe holds, so that publication of the work item is withheld.

129. A non-transitory computer-readable medium storing instructions that, when executed by one or more processors, cause the one or more processors to perform the method of claim 127.

130. The method of claim 127, wherein the gate probe fails closed when it is unavailable, and a work item carrying the gate label stays in the backlog under its milestone without creating an owner card until the gate probe holds.

131. The method of claim 127, wherein, for a business function having a step that is irreversible and identity-bound, a preparation work item is executed by a fleet of agents and an owner card is created having a prepared package, a default in effect and a closing probe, the owner card being closed when the closing probe observes the step.

132. The method of claim 127, wherein the consistency check extracts load-bearing statements from each of the plurality of artifacts, aligns the statements by key, and lists as mismatches statements that disagree between artifacts, statements required by the artifact schema but absent, and statements whose built or prophetic status labels are inconsistent.

133. The method of claim 127, further comprising marking an artifact stale when a hash of the artifact differs from the hash recorded at review or when an artifact on which it depends changed after the review, and refusing a reviewed status when the model family of a reviewer equals the model family of the author of the artifact.

134. The method of claim 127, wherein a business function is an intellectual property function whose artifacts comprise an invention disclosure, a prior-art register, a specification with figures having reference numerals, claim-style summaries, a claim strategy memo, a filing checklist and an evidence log, the method further comprising preparing a filing-ready package and creating an owner card for a filing step to be performed by a person.

135. The method of claim 127, wherein each statement of a marketing artifact carries a disclosure tag selected from at least three tags, a first tag marking an outcome-level statement that may be published while the gate probe has not held, a second tag marking a statement that names a mechanism and waits for the gate probe, and a third tag marking a statement restricted to recipients under non-disclosure.

136. The method of claim 127, wherein the generating instantiates default business functions, replaces a business function lacking a stored definition with a generic business function selected by a stored mapping, creates only work items that do not yet exist when the idea file is amended, and proposes retirement of work items of an excluded business function.

137. The method of claim 127, further comprising storing each input of a principal received at any time as a record, classifying the input as a direction change, an answer to an open question, a correction of an artifact or a comment, re-running the generating on a direction change, applying a default in effect to each unanswered question, and creating an owner card only for an irreversible and identity-bound step for which no reversible form can be constructed, a number of owner cards shown being capped.

138. The method of claim 127, further comprising ingesting search and visit data for a complete past day into a signal store as rows each having a date, a source, an entity and a metric, and, when a credential is missing or refused, writing a ledger row with a reason and no value so that the metric is read as not connected and not as zero.

139. The method of claim 138, further comprising opening, for a page having impressions and a click-through below a threshold, a work item for a managing persona, with a prediction recorded before the work item is acted on.

# ABSTRACT

A system operates an organization through a hierarchy of virtual staff agents, each defined by a persona record with a role, a program, metrics and an enumerated set of permitted operations. On ticks a portfolio-manager agent allocates focus capacity across programs by risk, and program-manager agents rank work and write priority records that an execution queue uses to dispatch work to executor processes; each operation is recorded with its prior value. Each decision carries a machine-readable prediction that is scored; a trust ledger per surface and action class maps verified outcomes to an autonomy ladder that selects each action's execution path. A plus loop reserves capacity for evidence-driven, additive changes to working products and keeps a change only when its outcome improves without guard regression. Only irreversible, identity-bound steps reach the human principal. Changes run under fenced claims, two-family review and probe-triggered revert; policy amends itself from recurring output signatures.
